          ARP4754A REQUIREMENTS VALIDATION REPORT
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

ANALYSIS METADATA
─────────────────
  Standard              : ARP4754A v3.0
  Project               : Synthetic Aircraft Requirements Dataset
  Purpose               : Demonstrate ARP4754A rule violations, including multi-requirement consistency issues
  Requirements analysed : 27
  RAG knowledge base    : NOT LOADED
  Indexed source docs   : none

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
─────────────────
The analysis of the Synthetic Aircraft Requirements Dataset reveals significant compliance issues with ARP4754A standards. Critical contradictions were identified, notably between REQ-002 and REQ-003 (Rule-K01) regarding response times, and REQ-012 and REQ-013 (Rule-K04) concerning failure classifications. These inconsistencies, along with terminology and unit discrepancies, undermine the requirements' clarity and feasibility. The validation status is FAIL due to unresolved blocking issues and critical inconsistencies.

OVERALL COMPLIANCE SCORE: 45/100
  Weighted: Completeness 25% + Verifiability 25% + Consistency 20%
          + Correctness 15% + Traceability 15%

COMPLIANCE BREAKDOWN
────────────────────
  §5.2 Correctness        : 50/100
  §5.3 Completeness       : 60/100
  §5.4 Consistency        : 30/100
  §5.5 Verifiability      : 55/100
  §5.6 Traceability       : 50/100
  Wording & Morphology    : 65/100

BLOCKING FINDINGS  (must resolve before approval)
──────────────────────────────────────────────────
  REQ-002 | RULE-K01 | Contradictory response time with REQ-003
  REQ-012 | RULE-K04 | Inconsistent failure classification with REQ-013

CRITICAL FINDINGS  (HIGH severity)
───────────────────────────────────
  REQ-008 | RULE-K03 | Inconsistent altitude accuracy units with REQ-009
  REQ-015 | RULE-K06 | Conflicting update rates with REQ-016

MAJOR FINDINGS  (MEDIUM severity)
──────────────────────────────────
  REQ-001 | RULE-K02 | Ambiguous terminology compared to REQ-002 and REQ-003
  REQ-025 | RULE-K05 | Redundant pitch rate limit with REQ-026

MINOR FINDINGS  (LOW severity)
────────────────────────────────
  None detected

PRIORITISED ACTION PLAN
────────────────────────
1. [BLOCKING] REQ-002 RULE-K01 — Resolve response time contradiction with REQ-003.
2. [BLOCKING] REQ-012 RULE-K04 — Align failure classification with REQ-013.
3. [HIGH] REQ-008 RULE-K03 — Standardize altitude accuracy units with REQ-009.
4. [HIGH] REQ-015 RULE-K06 — Harmonize update rates with REQ-016.
5. [MEDIUM] REQ-001 RULE-K02 — Clarify terminology to align with REQ-002 and REQ-003.
6. [MEDIUM] REQ-025 RULE-K05 — Eliminate redundancy with REQ-026.

VALIDATION STATUS: FAIL
The requirement set fails due to unresolved blocking contradictions and critical inconsistencies that compromise compliance with ARP4754A standards. Immediate corrective actions are necessary to address these issues.

  ┌─ §5.3 COMPLETENESS
  │  To perform a completeness analysis on the given requirement, we need to evaluate various aspects to ensure that the requirement is fully defined and actionable. Here is a breakdown of the completeness analysis for the requirement "Flight Control Response Speed":
  │  
  │  1. **Requirement ID and Title:**
  │     - **ID:** REQ-001
  │     - **Title:** Flight Control Response Speed
  │     - The requirement has a unique identifier and a descriptive title, which is essential for traceability and reference.
  │  
  │  2. **Requirement Text:**
  │     - **Text:** "The Flight Control System shall respond rapidly to pilot inputs."
  │     - The requirement specifies a performance aspect of the Flight Control System, focusing on response speed.
  │  
  │  3. **Verification Method:**
  │     - **Verification Method:** Not provided.
  │     - **Analysis:** The requirement lacks a defined verification method. This is a critical gap, as it is unclear how compliance with the requirement will be demonstrated. A verification method such as testing, analysis, inspection, or demonstration should be specified.
  │  
  │  4. **Rationale:**
  │     - **Rationale:** "To ensure good handling qualities."
  │     - The rationale provides a reason for the requirement, linking it to the overall goal of ensuring good handling qualities. This helps in understanding the importance of the requirement.
  │  
  │  5. **Attributes:**
  │     - **Has Identifier:** True
  │     - **Has Verification Method:** False
  │     - **Has Source:** True
  │     - **Has DAL (Design Assurance Level):** False
  │     - **Modal Verb:** "shall"
  │     - **Type:** Performance
  │  
  │  6. **Ambiguity:**
  │     - **Ambiguous Terms Found:** "rapidly," "good"
  │     - **Analysis:** The terms "rapidly" and "good" are subjective and lack quantifiable measures. This ambiguity can lead to different interpretations and should be clarified with specific metrics or thresholds.
  │  
  │  7. **Compound Requirement:**
  │     - **Compound:** False
  │     - The requirement is not compound, meaning it focuses on a single aspect, which is beneficial for clarity and focus.
  │  
  │  **Recommendations for Completeness:**
  │  
  │  1. **Define Verification Method:** Specify how the requirement will be verified. For example, "The response time of the Flight Control System shall be tested to ensure it is less than X milliseconds following pilot input."
  │  
  │  2. **Clarify Ambiguous Terms:** Replace "rapidly" and "good" with quantifiable metrics. For example, "The Flight Control System shall respond within X milliseconds to pilot inputs to ensure handling qualities meet standard Y."
  │  
  │  3. **Consider Design Assurance Level (DAL):** If applicable, specify the Design Assurance Level to indicate the criticality and safety requirements associated with the system.
  │  
  │  By addressing these gaps, the requirement can be made more complete, clear, and verifiable.

  ┌─ §5.5 VERIFIABILITY
  │  To perform a verifiability analysis on the given requirement, we need to assess whether the requirement can be verified through objective means. This involves examining the clarity, measurability, and testability of the requirement. Let's break down the analysis:
  │  
  │  1. **Clarity and Ambiguity**:
  │     - The requirement uses ambiguous terms such as "rapidly" and "good," which are subjective and open to interpretation. These terms need to be defined with specific, measurable criteria to ensure clarity.
  │  
  │  2. **Measurability**:
  │     - The requirement lacks specific performance metrics or thresholds. To be verifiable, it should specify a quantifiable response time (e.g., "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs within 0.5 seconds").
  │  
  │  3. **Testability**:
  │     - Without a defined verification method, it is unclear how the requirement will be tested or demonstrated. A suitable verification method could be "testing" or "demonstration," where the response time is measured under controlled conditions.
  │  
  │  4. **Verification Method**:
  │     - The requirement currently lacks a verification method. It should include a method such as "inspection," "analysis," "demonstration," or "testing" to indicate how compliance will be verified.
  │  
  │  5. **Performance Type**:
  │     - As a performance requirement, it should focus on measurable outcomes. The requirement should specify the conditions under which the response speed is measured (e.g., during normal flight operations, under specific environmental conditions).
  │  
  │  6. **Acceptance Criteria**:
  │     - Define clear acceptance criteria that outline what constitutes a "pass" or "fail" for the requirement. This could include specific response times and conditions.
  │  
  │  7. **Rationale**:
  │     - The rationale provided ("To ensure good handling qualities") supports the need for the requirement but does not contribute to its verifiability. It is important to maintain the rationale for context but focus on measurable aspects for verification.
  │  
  │  8. **Improvement Suggestions**:
  │     - Revise the requirement to include specific, measurable criteria. For example: "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs within 0.5 seconds to ensure good handling qualities."
  │     - Specify the verification method, such as "The response time shall be verified through testing under simulated flight conditions."
  │  
  │  By addressing these points, the requirement can be made verifiable, ensuring that it can be objectively tested and validated.

  │     - The verification method is appropriate for this type of performance requirement. Testing is a direct way to verify that the system meets the specified response time.

  │     - The requirement includes a clear performance threshold (20 ms), which serves as the acceptance criterion for the test. This threshold is essential for determining whether the system meets the requirement.

  ┌─ §5.2 CORRECTNESS
  │  To perform a correctness analysis on the given requirement, we need to evaluate several aspects to ensure that the requirement is clear, feasible, and testable. Let's break down the analysis:
  │  
  │  1. **Clarity and Specificity**:
  │     - The requirement is clear and specific. It states that the Flight Control System must respond to pilot inputs within 20 milliseconds in normal mode. There are no ambiguous terms found, which indicates that the requirement is well-defined.
  │  
  │  2. **Feasibility**:
  │     - The requirement specifies a response time of 20 milliseconds. To assess feasibility, it would be necessary to consider the current technology and system capabilities. If the system's hardware and software can support this response time, then the requirement is feasible. However, this analysis assumes that the 20 ms response time is achievable with the current or planned system architecture.
  │  
  │  3. **Testability**:
  │     - The requirement is testable, as indicated by the verification method being "Test." This means that the system's response time can be measured and verified against the 20 ms criterion during testing.
  │  
  │  4. **Completeness**:
  │     - The requirement includes an identifier (REQ-002), a title, and a rationale, which adds context and justification for the requirement. However, it lacks a Design Assurance Level (DAL), which might be necessary for safety-critical systems like flight control systems. Including a DAL would help determine the level of rigor required in the development and verification processes.
  │  
  │  5. **Consistency**:
  │     - The requirement uses the modal verb "shall," which is appropriate for mandatory requirements. There are no compound statements, which helps maintain clarity and focus.
  │  
  │  6. **Source and Rationale**:
  │     - The requirement has a rationale, "Derived from handling quality objectives," which provides a basis for why the requirement exists. However, the source of these handling quality objectives is not explicitly mentioned, which could be important for traceability and validation purposes.
  │  
  │  7. **Assumptions and Constraints**:
  │     - The requirement assumes that the system operates in "normal mode," but it does not define what constitutes normal mode or how it differs from other modes. This could be a potential area for clarification to ensure that the requirement is interpreted correctly.
  │  
  │  In summary, the requirement is generally well-structured and clear, with a focus on performance. It is testable and has a rationale, but it could benefit from additional details regarding the source of the handling quality objectives and the inclusion of a Design Assurance Level. Additionally, clarifying what is meant by "normal mode" could enhance understanding and implementation.

  │  To perform a correctness analysis on the given requirement, we need to evaluate several aspects, including clarity, completeness, verifiability, and consistency. Here's an analysis of the requirement "The Flight Control System shall be highly reliable and robust under all conditions":
  │  
  │  1. **Clarity**:
  │     - The requirement uses ambiguous terms such as "highly," "reliable," and "robust." These terms are subjective and can be interpreted differently by different stakeholders. For clarity, these terms should be defined with specific metrics or standards that quantify what "highly reliable" and "robust" mean in the context of the Flight Control System.
  │  
  │  2. **Completeness**:
  │     - The requirement is incomplete in terms of verification. It lacks a verification method, which is crucial for determining how the requirement will be tested or validated. Without a verification method, it is unclear how compliance with the requirement will be demonstrated.
  │  
  │  3. **Verifiability**:
  │     - Due to the use of ambiguous terms and the absence of a verification method, the requirement is not currently verifiable. To make it verifiable, the requirement should specify measurable criteria or conditions under which the system's reliability and robustness will be assessed.
  │  
  │  4. **Consistency**:
  │     - The requirement appears to be consistent with its rationale, which is to improve overall system safety. However, without specific criteria or standards, it is difficult to ensure consistency with other related requirements or industry standards.
  │  
  │  5. **Rationale**:
  │     - The rationale provided is clear and aligns with the safety type of the requirement. Improving system safety is a valid and important goal for a Flight Control System.
  │  
  │  6. **Identification and Traceability**:
  │     - The requirement has an identifier ("REQ-005") and a title ("Flight Control Reliability"), which aids in traceability and management within a larger requirements document.
  │  
  │  7. **Source and DAL**:
  │     - The requirement has a source, which is important for understanding the origin and context of the requirement. However, it lacks a Design Assurance Level (DAL), which is often critical in safety-related systems to determine the level of rigor required in development and verification processes.
  │  
  │  **Recommendations for Improvement**:
  │  - Define specific metrics or standards for "highly reliable" and "robust" to remove ambiguity.
  │  - Include a verification method to specify how the requirement will be tested or validated.
  │  - Consider specifying a Design Assurance Level (DAL) if applicable, to align with industry standards for safety-critical systems.
  │  - Ensure that the requirement is consistent with other related requirements and standards.
  │  
  │  By addressing these points, the requirement can be made clearer, more complete, and verifiable, ultimately contributing to the overall safety and reliability of the Flight Control System.

  ┌─ §5.5 VERIFIABILITY
  │  To perform a verifiability analysis on the given requirement "REQ-007: Autopilot Altitude Control," we need to assess whether the requirement can be verified through specific methods and if it is clearly defined to allow for effective testing. Here are the key aspects to consider:
  │  
  │  1. **Clarity and Specificity**:
  │     - The requirement states that the "Autopilot shall maintain altitude accurately." The term "accurately" is identified as ambiguous. For verifiability, this term needs to be quantified. For example, specifying a tolerance range (e.g., ±50 feet) or a percentage deviation from the set altitude would make the requirement more precise and testable.
  │  
  │  2. **Verification Method**:
  │     - The requirement currently lacks a specified verification method. Common verification methods include inspection, demonstration, analysis, and testing. For this requirement, testing would likely be the most appropriate method, where the autopilot system is evaluated under various conditions to ensure it maintains the specified altitude within the defined tolerance.
  │  
  │  3. **Performance Criteria**:
  │     - As a performance requirement, it should include measurable criteria. Once "accurately" is defined with specific metrics, these criteria can be used to assess whether the autopilot system meets the requirement.
  │  
  │  4. **Testability**:
  │     - The requirement must be testable. By defining "accurately" with measurable terms, the requirement becomes testable. Test scenarios can be designed to simulate different flight conditions (e.g., turbulence, varying aircraft weights) to verify the autopilot's performance.
  │  
  │  5. **Acceptance Criteria**:
  │     - Establish clear acceptance criteria based on the defined accuracy metrics. For example, the autopilot should maintain altitude within ±50 feet of the target altitude for 95% of the flight duration under specified conditions.
  │  
  │  6. **Performance Thresholds and Tolerances**:
  │     - Define performance thresholds and tolerances that the autopilot system must meet. This includes specifying the acceptable range of altitude deviation and the conditions under which these thresholds apply.
  │  
  │  7. **Inspection and Demonstration**:
  │     - While testing is the primary method, inspection and demonstration can also play roles. Inspection can ensure the system's components are correctly installed and configured, while demonstration can show the system's capability in a controlled environment.
  │  
  │  In summary, to enhance the verifiability of the requirement, it is essential to:
  │  - Define "accurately" with specific, measurable terms.
  │  - Specify a verification method, such as testing.
  │  - Establish clear performance criteria and acceptance criteria.
  │  - Define performance thresholds and tolerances.

This requirement is a cornerstone for maintaining the integrity and safety of the aircraft's flight control capabilities.

  ┌─ §5.2 CORRECTNESS
  │  To perform a correctness analysis on the given requirement, we need to evaluate several aspects to ensure that it is well-defined, unambiguous, and feasible. Let's break down the requirement:
  │  
  │  1. **Requirement Structure and Clarity:**
  │     - **ID and Title:** The requirement has a clear identifier ("REQ-013") and a descriptive title ("Flight Control System Failure Classification and DAL"), which helps in tracking and referencing.
  │     - **Text:** The requirement states, "Loss of the Flight Control System shall be classified as Catastrophic and assigned DAL A." This is a clear and concise statement specifying the classification and Design Assurance Level (DAL) for a specific failure condition.
  │  
  │  2. **Verification Method:**
  │     - The requirement specifies "Safety Analysis" as the verification method. This is appropriate for a safety-related requirement, as safety analysis techniques (such as Fault Tree Analysis or Failure Modes and Effects Analysis) are typically used to assess and verify safety classifications and DAL assignments.
  │  
  │  3. **Rationale:**
  │     - The rationale provided is "Functional hazard assessment," which is a standard practice in safety-critical systems to determine the impact of failures and to classify them accordingly. This rationale supports the requirement's classification decision.

  │     - **Has Identifier:** True, as the requirement has a unique ID.
  │     - **Has Verification Method:** True, as "Safety Analysis" is specified.

  │     - **Has DAL:** True, as DAL A is specified.

  │

  │     - **Ambiguous Terms:** None found, indicating clarity in the language used.

  │

  │     - The requirement is correct in the context of safety-critical systems, where the loss of a flight control system is typically considered catastrophic due to the potential for loss of aircraft and life.

It correctly identifies the severity and assurance level for the loss of the flight control system, using standard safety analysis methods for verification. However, it would benefit from explicitly stating the source or reference to ensure traceability and alignment with higher-level safety requirements or standards.

  │  
  │  6. **Ambiguity**: No ambiguous terms are identified, indicating that the requirement is straightforward and unambiguous.
  │

  │       - Simulation under various environmental conditions.
  │       - Analysis of system performance data in operational environments.
  │  
  │  4. **Rationale:**
  │     - **Rationale Provided:** Normal operational expectations.
  │     - **Justification:** Ensures the system meets expected performance levels under all specified environmental conditions, aligning with industry standards and safety requirements.

  │       - Detailed performance metrics (e.g., response time, accuracy) under these conditions.

  │       - System safety requirements that ensure the system remains safe under environmental stress.

  │     - **PSSA (Preliminary System Safety Assessment):** Assess preliminary safety measures to mitigate identified hazards.

  ┌─ §5.2 CORRECTNESS
  │  Performing a correctness analysis on the requirement "REQ-021" involves evaluating its clarity, completeness, and testability. Let's break down the requirement based on these criteria:
  │  
  │  1. **Clarity**:
  │     - **Title**: "Environmental Operation" is somewhat misleading as it suggests the requirement pertains to environmental conditions, whereas the text refers to the operation of the Flight Control System.
  │     - **Text**: "The Flight Control System shall operate correctly." This statement is vague and lacks specificity. It does not define what "operate correctly" means, which can lead to multiple interpretations.
  │  
  │  2. **Completeness**:
  │     - The requirement lacks details on what constitutes correct operation. It should specify the conditions or criteria under which the system is considered to be operating correctly.
  │     - There is no context or scope provided, such as environmental conditions, operational modes, or performance metrics.
  │  
  │  3. **Testability**:
  │     - Without a clear definition of "operate correctly," it is challenging to devise tests to verify this requirement. Testability requires specific, measurable criteria that can be evaluated through testing or inspection.
  │  
  │  4. **Verification Method**:
  │     - The requirement lacks a verification method, which is crucial for determining how compliance will be assessed. This omission makes it difficult to plan for testing or validation activities.
  │  
  │  5. **Rationale**:
  │     - The rationale provided, "Normal operational expectations," is not sufficient to justify the requirement. It does not explain why the requirement is necessary or what specific operational expectations are being referred to.
  │  
  │  6. **Other Considerations**:
  │     - **Ambiguity**: Although no ambiguous terms are explicitly listed, "operate correctly" is inherently ambiguous.
  │     - **Compound**: The requirement is not compound, which is positive as it focuses on a single aspect of the system.
  │     - **Modal Verb**: The use of "shall" indicates a mandatory requirement, which is appropriate for a system requirement.
  │  
  │  **Recommendations for Improvement**:
  │  - Define what "operate correctly" means in terms of specific performance metrics, conditions, or scenarios.
  │  - Include a verification method to outline how the requirement will be tested or validated.
  │  - Provide a more detailed rationale that explains the necessity of the requirement and its impact on system performance.
  │  - Consider revising the title to better reflect the content of the requirement, such as "Flight Control System Operational Performance."
  │  
  │  By addressing these issues, the requirement can be made clearer, more complete, and testable, ensuring it effectively guides the development and verification of the Flight Control System.

  ┌─ WORDING & MORPHOLOGY

  │

For an environmental requirement, it might be expected to include specific environmental conditions (e.g., temperature ranges, humidity levels) under which the system should function.
  │  
  │  3. **Consistency**: The requirement uses the modal verb "shall," which is appropriate for mandatory requirements. However, without a clear definition of "correctly," it is difficult to assess consistency with other requirements.
  │  
  │  4. **Testability**: The requirement lacks a verification method, which makes it difficult to determine how to test or verify that the system operates correctly. This impacts the ability to ensure compliance.
  │

  │  
  │  ### Morphology Analysis
  │  
  │  1. **Sentence Structure**: The sentence is simple and straightforward, consisting of a subject ("The Flight Control System"), a modal verb ("shall"), and a predicate ("operate correctly"). 
  │  
  │  2. **Modal Verb**: The use of "shall" indicates a requirement that is mandatory. This is appropriate for requirements but needs to be supported by clear and specific criteria.
  │  
  │  3. **Noun Phrase**: "The Flight Control System" is a clear and specific noun phrase, assuming that it is defined elsewhere in the documentation.
  │  
  │  4. **Verb Phrase**: "Operate correctly" is the verb phrase. The verb "operate" is clear, but the adverb "correctly" is not well-defined in this context.
  │

  │  
  │  ### Recommendations

The system's performance can be monitored to ensure it operates correctly at both the lower and upper temperature limits, as well as at intermediate points.

  │  6. **Ambiguity**: No ambiguous terms are identified in the requirement. Terms like "Flight Control System," "software," "limit," "pitch rate," and "deg/s" are standard and well-understood in the context of aerospace engineering.
  │  
  │  7. **Compound Nature**: The requirement is not compound, as it addresses a single aspect of the system's performance.
  │  
  │  ### Morphology Analysis
  │  
  │  1. **Nouns**: 
  │     - "Flight Control System": Refers to the system responsible for controlling the aircraft's flight dynamics.
  │     - "software": Indicates the software component of the Flight Control System.
  │     - "pitch rate": A specific parameter related to the aircraft's orientation.
  │     - "deg/s": Abbreviation for degrees per second, a unit of angular velocity.
  │  
  │  2. **Verbs**:
  │     - "shall limit": The verb phrase indicating the action that the software must perform.
  │  
  │  3. **Adjectives**:
  │     - "5 deg/s": Functions as an adjective phrase specifying the limit for the pitch rate.
  │  
  │  4. **Prepositions**:
  │     - "to": Used to indicate the limit value for the pitch rate.
  │  
  │  5. **Structure**: The sentence structure is straightforward, with a clear subject ("The Flight Control System software"), verb ("shall limit"), and object ("pitch rate to 5 deg/s").
  │  
  │  Overall, the requirement is well-structured and free from ambiguity, with precise terminology and a clear performance specification.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  §5.4 CONSISTENCY FINDINGS  (cross-requirement — bulk analysis)
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
─────────────────────────────────────────────────
REQ-K01 [BLOCKING] — Contradiction Analysis
  - Contradiction between REQ-002 and REQ-003: REQ-002 specifies a response time of 20 ms, while REQ-003 specifies a minimum response time of 30 ms.
  - Contradiction between REQ-012 and REQ-013: REQ-012 classifies loss of the Flight Control System as Major, while REQ-013 classifies it as Catastrophic.
  - Contradiction between REQ-015 and REQ-016: REQ-015 specifies an update rate of 10 ms, while REQ-016 specifies 15 ms.
  - Duplicate requirements: REQ-025 and REQ-026 both limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s.
REQ-K02 [MEDIUM] — Terminology Consistency
  - Inconsistent use of terms: "rapidly" in REQ-001 is ambiguous compared to specific timing in REQ-002 and REQ-003.
  - Terms like "highly reliable" in REQ-005 and "accurately" in REQ-007 are subjective and lack quantifiable definitions.
REQ-K03 [HIGH] — Units and Tolerances
  - Inconsistent altitude accuracy units: REQ-008 uses ±50 ft, while REQ-009 uses ±20 m. These are not equivalent (±50 ft ≈ ±15.24 m).
REQ-K04 [BLOCKING] — DAL Consistency
  - Inconsistency in DAL assignment: REQ-012 and REQ-013 have conflicting classifications for the same failure condition, affecting DAL assignment.
REQ-K05 [HIGH] — Performance Feasibility
  - Potential infeasibility between REQ-015 and REQ-016 due to conflicting actuator command update rates (10 ms vs. 15 ms).
REQ-K06 [HIGH] — Timing Consistency
  - Conflicting timing constraints: REQ-002 (20 ms response) vs. REQ-003 (minimum 30 ms response) and REQ-015 (10 ms update rate) vs. REQ-016 (15 ms update rate).

─────────────────────────────────────────────────
SUMMARY: The requirement set contains several critical contradictions, particularly in response times and failure classifications, which must be resolved before approval. Terminology inconsistencies and unit mismatches also need addressing to ensure clarity and compliance. Performance and timing conflicts suggest potential feasibility issues that require further analysis. Overall, the requirement set fails the consistency audit due to these significant issues.

CONTRADICTIONS  [BLOCKING]
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-001  ×  REQ-003
  Detail   : REQ-001 requires the Flight Control System to respond to pilot inputs always, without a specific time constraint, while REQ-003 requires a response time of at least 30 ms in NORMAL mode. These requirements contradict each other in NORMAL mode, as one implies immediate response and the other imposes a minimum delay.
  Evidence : "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs always." vs "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs ≥ 30 ms in NORMAL mode."

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-002  ×  REQ-003
  Detail   : REQ-002 and REQ-003 specify contradictory constraints for the response time of the Flight Control System in NORMAL mode. REQ-002 requires a response within 20 ms, while REQ-003 requires a response time of at least 30 ms, which is logically incompatible.
  Evidence : "within 20 ms" from REQ-002 and "≥ 30 ms" from REQ-003

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-003  ×  REQ-004
  Detail   : The requirements specify contradictory constraints for the response time of the Flight Control System to pilot inputs. REQ-003 requires a response time of at least 30 ms in NORMAL mode, while REQ-004 requires a response time within 25 ms always. These constraints cannot be simultaneously satisfied.
  Evidence : "respond to pilot inputs ≥ 30 ms in NORMAL mode" vs "respond to pilot inputs within 25 ms always"

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-012  ×  REQ-013
  Detail   : The two requirements classify the loss of the Flight Control System differently, which is logically incompatible. REQ-012 classifies it as Major, while REQ-013 classifies it as Catastrophic and assigns DAL A. These classifications cannot coexist for the same event.
  Evidence : "be classified as Major on loss" vs "be classified as Catastrophic and assigned DAL A on loss"

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-012  ×  REQ-021
  Detail   : REQ-012 and REQ-021 present a logical contradiction. REQ-012 allows for the loss of the Flight Control System to be classified as Major, implying that loss is possible. In contrast, REQ-021 requires the system to operate correctly always, which implies no loss should occur.
  Evidence : "Loss of the Flight Control System shall be classified as Major on loss." vs "The Flight Control System shall operate correctly always."

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-015  ×  REQ-016
  Detail   : REQ-015 and REQ-016 specify different update rates for the same action under the same conditions. REQ-015 requires updates every 10 ms, while REQ-016 requires updates every 15 ms, which are logically incompatible.
  Evidence : "every 10 ms" from REQ-015 and "every 15 ms" from REQ-016

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-015  ×  REQ-017
  Detail   : REQ-015 specifies a fixed update rate of every 10 ms, whereas REQ-017 allows updates 'as required', which could be more or less frequent. These requirements are incompatible as they prescribe different update strategies for the same action.
  Evidence : "every 10 ms" from REQ-015 and "as required" from REQ-017

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-018  ×  REQ-020
  Detail   : REQ-018 requires data exchange with the Autopilot without specifying a protocol, while REQ-020 mandates using AFDX with a maximum latency of 5 ms. These requirements are contradictory because they specify different communication protocols and constraints for the same data exchange operation.
  Evidence : "exchange data with Autopilot always" (REQ-018) vs "exchange data with Autopilot using AFDX with a maximum latency of 5 ms always" (REQ-020)

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-019  ×  REQ-020
  Detail   : REQ-019 and REQ-020 specify different data exchange protocols for the Flight Control System with the Autopilot, both applicable 'always'. This creates a logical conflict as the system cannot simultaneously use ARINC 429 and AFDX for the same function.
  Evidence : "using ARINC 429 always" (REQ-019) vs "using AFDX with a maximum latency of 5 ms always" (REQ-020)

  [BLOCKING]  REQ-008  ×  REQ-009
  Detail   : The two requirements specify different constraints for altitude maintenance during CRUISE mode. REQ-008 requires maintaining altitude within ±50 ft, while REQ-009 requires it within ±20 m. These constraints are not equivalent, leading to a contradiction in the required performance of the Autopilot.
  Evidence : "within ±50 ft" (REQ-008) vs "within ±20 m" (REQ-009)

OVERLAPS        [HIGH]
──────────────────────────────────────────────────

  [HIGH]  REQ-001  ×  REQ-002
  Detail   : REQ-001 requires the Flight Control System to respond to pilot inputs always, without specifying a time constraint, while REQ-002 specifies a response time of within 20 ms in NORMAL mode. This creates an overlap where both requirements apply in NORMAL mode, but with different levels of specificity regarding response time.
  Evidence : "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs always." vs "The Flight Control System shall respond to pilot inputs within 20 ms in NORMAL mode."

  [HIGH]  REQ-001  ×  REQ-021
  Detail   : Both requirements pertain to the Flight Control System and apply universally across all modes. REQ-001 focuses on responding to pilot inputs, while REQ-021 emphasizes correct operation. The overlap arises because responding to inputs is a subset of operating correctly, leading to potential ambiguity in implementation.
  Evidence : "respond to pilot inputs always" (REQ-001), "operate correctly always" (REQ-021)

  [HIGH]  REQ-005  ×  REQ-014
  Detail   : Both REQ-005 and REQ-014 pertain to the overall performance and safety of the Flight Control System under all conditions. REQ-005 emphasizes reliability and robustness, while REQ-014 focuses on meeting safety objectives. The overlap in scope and intent could lead to ambiguity in implementation.
  Evidence : "be reliable and robust under all conditions" vs "meet safety objectives always"

  [HIGH]  REQ-005  ×  REQ-022
  Detail   : REQ-005 demands reliability and robustness under all conditions, while REQ-022 specifies correct operation within a temperature range. The overlap occurs because both address operational correctness, but REQ-022 introduces a specific environmental constraint not covered by REQ-005.
  Evidence : "be reliable and robust under all conditions" (REQ-005) and "operate correctly between −40°C and +70°C always" (REQ-022)

  [HIGH]  REQ-014  ×  REQ-021
  Detail   : Both requirements pertain to the Flight Control System and apply universally across all modes. They overlap in scope as both imply a need for correct operation, but one emphasizes safety objectives while the other emphasizes correct operation. This creates ambiguity about the priority or relationship between safety and correctness.
  Evidence : "meet safety objectives always" vs "operate correctly always"

  [HIGH]  REQ-014  ×  REQ-022
  Detail   : Both requirements apply to the Flight Control System universally, but REQ-022 specifies a temperature constraint for correct operation. While REQ-014 focuses on safety objectives, the overlap arises because both imply correct operation under all conditions, including temperature, without clarifying the relationship between safety and operational correctness.
  Evidence : "meet safety objectives always" vs "operate correctly between −40°C and +70°C always"

  [HIGH]  REQ-016  ×  REQ-017
  Detail   : Both requirements pertain to the update frequency of actuator commands in the Flight Control System. REQ-016 specifies a fixed update interval of 15 ms, while REQ-017 allows for updates 'as required'. This creates ambiguity about the correct update frequency, as the conditions for 'as required' are not defined.
  Evidence : "update actuator commands every 15 ms" vs "update actuator commands as required"

  [HIGH]  REQ-018  ×  REQ-019
  Detail   : Both requirements specify that the Flight Control System shall exchange data with the Autopilot always, but REQ-019 adds a constraint on the communication protocol (ARINC 429). This creates ambiguity about whether the protocol constraint is mandatory or optional, as REQ-018 does not specify any protocol.
  Evidence : "exchange data with Autopilot always" (REQ-018) vs "exchange data with Autopilot using ARINC 429 always" (REQ-019)

  [HIGH]  REQ-007  ×  REQ-010
  Detail   : Both requirements involve the Autopilot maintaining altitude, but REQ-010 adds a constraint of maintaining within ±50 ft and includes an additional action to disconnect when sensor data is invalid. This creates ambiguity about the conditions under which each requirement applies, especially regarding the altitude constraint.
  Evidence : "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude always." vs "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude within ±50 ft and disconnect automatically when sensor data is invalid."

  [HIGH]  REQ-008  ×  REQ-010
  Detail   : Both requirements involve maintaining altitude within ±50 ft, but REQ-010 adds an additional condition for automatic disconnection when sensor data is invalid. This creates an overlap in the altitude maintenance requirement, with REQ-010 applying universally and REQ-008 specifically to CRUISE mode, potentially causing ambiguity in implementation.
  Evidence : "maintain altitude within ±50 ft during CRUISE mode" (REQ-008) vs "maintain altitude within ±50 ft and disconnect automatically when sensor data is invalid" (REQ-010)

  [HIGH]  REQ-009  ×  REQ-010
  Detail   : Both requirements involve maintaining altitude, but REQ-009 specifies a constraint of ±20 m during cruise mode, while REQ-010 specifies ±50 ft for all modes. This creates ambiguity about which altitude constraint applies during cruise mode, as the two constraints are not equivalent.
  Evidence : "maintain altitude within ±20 m during CRUISE mode" vs "maintain altitude within ±50 ft"

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-001  ×  REQ-004
  Detail   : REQ-004 specifies a response time constraint of 25 ms, which inherently includes the requirement of REQ-001 for the system to respond always. Satisfying REQ-004 automatically satisfies REQ-001, as responding within 25 ms is a stricter condition than simply responding always.
  Evidence : "respond to pilot inputs always" (REQ-001) and "respond to pilot inputs within 25 ms always" (REQ-004)

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-002  ×  REQ-004
  Detail   : REQ-004's constraint of responding within 25 ms always subsumes REQ-002's constraint of responding within 20 ms in NORMAL mode. Satisfying REQ-004 inherently satisfies REQ-002, making REQ-002 redundant.
  Evidence : "within 20 ms in NORMAL mode" from REQ-002 and "within 25 ms always" from REQ-004

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-005  ×  REQ-021
  Detail   : REQ-005 requires the Flight Control System to be reliable and robust under all conditions, which inherently includes operating correctly always as stated in REQ-021. Thus, REQ-021 is fully subsumed by REQ-005.
  Evidence : "be reliable and robust under all conditions" (REQ-005) and "operate correctly always" (REQ-021)

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-015  ×  REQ-021
  Detail   : REQ-021 is a broad requirement that the Flight Control System shall operate correctly always, which implicitly includes the specific behavior described in REQ-015. Satisfying the specific update rate in REQ-015 contributes to the overall correct operation required by REQ-021.
  Evidence : "update actuator commands every 10 ms always" vs "operate correctly always"

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-021  ×  REQ-022
  Detail   : REQ-022 is a more specific case of REQ-021. While REQ-021 requires the system to operate correctly always, REQ-022 specifies operation within a temperature range, which is a subset of 'always'. Thus, satisfying REQ-022 inherently satisfies REQ-021.
  Evidence : "operate correctly always" vs "operate correctly between −40°C and +70°C always"

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-025  ×  REQ-026
  Detail   : Both requirements specify the same constraint on the pitch rate of the Flight Control System, with identical conditions and constraints. REQ-026 is fully subsumed by REQ-025, as they are identical in scope and content.
  Evidence : "limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s always" vs "limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s always"

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-007  ×  REQ-008
  Detail   : REQ-007 requires the Autopilot to maintain altitude at all times, which inherently includes maintaining altitude during CRUISE mode. REQ-008 specifies a constraint of ±50 ft during CRUISE mode, but this is a subset of the broader requirement in REQ-007. Thus, satisfying REQ-007 automatically satisfies REQ-008.
  Evidence : "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude always." vs "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude within ±50 ft during CRUISE mode."

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-007  ×  REQ-009
  Detail   : REQ-007 mandates that the Autopilot maintain altitude at all times, which encompasses the requirement in REQ-009 to maintain altitude within ±20 m during CRUISE mode. The specific constraint in REQ-009 is a subset of the broader requirement in REQ-007, making REQ-009 redundant.
  Evidence : "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude always." vs "The Autopilot shall maintain altitude within ±20 m during CRUISE mode."

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-010  ×  REQ-011
  Detail   : REQ-010 includes the same disconnection condition as REQ-011, making REQ-011 redundant. Satisfying REQ-010, which covers both altitude maintenance and disconnection, inherently satisfies the disconnection requirement of REQ-011.
  Evidence : "disconnect automatically when sensor data is invalid" in both requirements

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-025  ×  REQ-027
  Detail   : Both requirements specify the same constraint on pitch rate limitation for the Flight Control System, with REQ-027 being a more specific instance of REQ-025. The software-specific requirement (REQ-027) is fully subsumed by the broader system-level requirement (REQ-025).
  Evidence : "limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s always" in both requirements

  [MEDIUM]  REQ-026  ×  REQ-027

  Evidence : "The Flight Control System shall limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s always." vs "The Flight Control System software shall limit pitch rate to 5 deg/s always."
